        Idea:
Build MegaLoop — a minimal iterative agent loop inside the megaplan codebase.

## What it is

MegaLoop automates the run → observe → diagnose → fix → run again cycle. It's NOT a literal while loop — it's an agentic process where the loop state machine drives the agent through phases, and sub-agents do the deep work. The main thread stays high-level: reading summaries, updating the plan, dispatching workers.

## Key design requirements

1. Two phases: plan and execute, cycling. The first plan phase captures the loop spec (goal, command, success criteria, allowed changes, optimization strategy, bug-finding approach, philosophy). Subsequent plan phases UPDATE the spec based on what the last execution found. Execute runs the command, observes results, spawns sub-agents to investigate/fix, and produces structured iteration results. Then back to plan.

2. State machine grounding. The loop spec is a living document — it captures the goal, how we measure success, what strategies to use, known issues, tried-and-failed approaches, current best results. This is continuously updated as we iterate. It's the compressed context that makes iteration 50 as informed as iteration 1.

3. Sub-agents for all deep work. The main thread orchestrates. Sub-agents investigate failures, fix code, compare results. Reuse megaplan's existing worker dispatch (run_step_with_worker with Claude/Codex/Hermes routing).

4. Strong bias to continue. The default is to keep going. Only pause for genuine decision points (risky changes, genuinely stuck, goal met).

5. Git integration. Commit before each fix. If results are worse, revert. Branch tip = best known state. Like autoresearch.

## What to learn from

- MEGALOOP.md (/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/MEGALOOP.md): The vision doc. Key patterns: setup conversation → structured spec, per-iteration structured results, conversation checkpoints, context management via the spec as compressed history, sub-agents for depth.

- autoresearch (https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch): Radical simplicity. One file to edit (train.py), one metric (val_bpb), git as experiment tracking, program.md as the agent's instructions. The loop: edit → run → measure → keep/revert → repeat. Key insight: the constraint IS the design. Fixed 5-minute time budget makes all experiments comparable.

- megaplan itself: Reuse the worker dispatch, structured artifacts, CLI patterns. But DON'T replicate the full state machine complexity — megaloop should be much simpler. Two phases cycling, not seven phases in a pipeline.

## Constraints

- Lives in the megaplan codebase (new megaloop/ package or megaplan/loop/ subpackage)
- Reuses megaplan's worker infrastructure (hermes_worker, codex, claude dispatch)
- Minimal new code — the intelligence comes from the LLM prompts, not complex Python
- The loop spec format should be simple (JSON)
- CLI: megaloop init, megaloop run, megaloop status, megaloop pause

## What I want from the plan

A concrete implementation plan that respects the actual complexity (state machine, sub-agent dispatch, structured artifacts, git integration) while keeping the code as small as possible. Two phases cycling. No over-engineering. Dig into both MEGALOOP.md and autoresearch to understand what patterns to steal and what to leave behind.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution guidance:\n\n**Parallelism:** T1, T2, T3 have no dependencies \u2014 execute them in parallel. T4 depends only on T1 (types). T6 depends only on T2 (schemas). These two can run as soon as their single dependency lands.\n\n**The critical path is:** T1 \u2192 T4 \u2192 T5 \u2192 T8 \u2192 T7 \u2192 T10. T5 (engine) is the largest piece and blocks most downstream work.\n\n**The #1 execution risk** is the permission check widening in T6. The gate identified this clearly: workers.py has THREE locations where `step == \"execute\"` gates permission flags. All three must become `step in (\"execute\", \"loop_execute\")`. Miss one and the loop agent silently runs without write permissions. Test this explicitly with both Claude and Codex routing.\n\n**Schema registration order matters:** STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES at workers.py:45 is used to derive _STEP_REQUIRED_KEYS at workers.py:57-60 at MODULE LOAD TIME. The SCHEMAS dict in schemas.py must have the `loop_plan.json` and `loop_execute.json` entries BEFORE the workers module tries to import them. Since workers.py imports SCHEMAS at the top level, both changes must land atomically or the import will crash with a KeyError.\n\n**Mock system quirk:** mock_worker_output at workers.py:600 has a special case for execute step (line 610-611) passing prompt_override. The loop mock handlers should follow the same pattern \u2014 check whether the _MOCK_DISPATCH handler signature needs prompt_override for loop_execute.\n\n**session_key_for fallthrough:** The function at workers.py:615-623 has explicit branches for plan/revise/critique steps. loop_plan and loop_execute will hit the default case. Verify the default produces unique keys (it likely uses f\"{agent}_{step}\" which is fine for loop steps since they're unique step names).\n\n**Keep it small:** The 800-line budget is real. The plan phase prompt and execute phase prompt together will be the largest pieces. Resist the urge to add elaborate error handling or logging \u2014 the intelligence is in the prompts, the Python is just plumbing."
}

\"\")),\n+        \"fix_description\": str(payload.get(\"fix_description\", \"\")),\n+        \"files_to_change\": _string_list(payload.get(\"files_to_change\")),\n+        \"confidence\": str(payload.get(\"confidence\", \"\")),\n+        \"should_pause\": bool(payload.get(\"should_pause\", False)),\n+        \"returncode\": int(final_command[\"returncode\"]),\n+        \"command_output\": str(final_command[\"output\"]),\n+        \"metric\": final_command.get(\"metric\"),\n+        \"commit_sha\": commit_sha or \"\",\n+        \"reverted\": reverted,\n+    }\n+    if \"reasoning\" in payload:\n+        result[\"reasoning\"] = str(payload[\"reasoning\"])\n+\n+    execution_artifact = {\n+        \"iteration\": iteration,\n+        \"baseline\": {\n+            \"returncode\": baseline[\"returncode\"],\n+            \"timed_out\": baseline[\"timed_out\"],\n+            \"metric\": baseline.get(\"metric\"),\n+            \"output\": baseline[\"output\"],\n+        },\n+        \"worker\": payload,\n+        \"final\": {\n+            \"returncode\": final_command[\"returncode\"],\n+            \"timed_out\": final_command[\"timed_out\"],\n+            \"metric\": final_command.get(\"metric\"),\n+            \"output\": final_command[\"output\"],\n+        },\n+        \"commit_sha\": commit_sha,\n+        \"reverted\": reverted,\n+    }\n+    atomic_write_json(current_iteration_artifact(plan_dir, \"loop_execute\", iteration), execution_artifact)\n+    atomic_write_text(current_iteration_raw_artifact(plan_dir, \"loop_execute\", iteration), worker.raw_output)\n+\n+    state.setdefault(\"results\", []).append(result)\n+    state[\"iteration\"] = iteration\n+    state[\"goal_met\"] = result[\"returncode\"] == 0\n+    if result[\"should_pause\"]:\n+        state[\"status\"] = \"paused\"\n+        state[\"pause_reason\"] = str(payload.get(\"outcome\") or \"Loop worker requested pause\")\n+    elif state[\"goal_met\"]:\n+        state[\"status\"] = \"done\"\n+    else:\n+        state[\"status\"] = \"running\"\n+    if _is_better(result, state.get(\"current_best\"), spec):\n+        state[\"current_best\"] = result\n+        if result.get(\"command_output\"):\n+            state[\"spec\"] = _merge_spec(spec, {\"best_result_summary\": result[\"command_output\"][:500]})\n+    state[\"phase\"] = \"plan\" if state[\"status\"] == \"running\" else state[\"phase\"]\n+    state[\"updated_at\"] = now_utc()\n+    return state\n+\n+\n+def should_continue(state: LoopState) -> bool:\n+    if state.get(\"status\") == \"paused\":\n+        return False\n+    if state.get(\"goal_met\"):\n+        return False\n+    if state.get(\"agent_requested_pause\"):\n+        return False\n+    max_iterations = int(state.get(\"max_iterations\", 0) or 0)\n+    if max_iterations and int(state.get(\"iteration\", 0)) >= max_iterations:\n+        return False\n+    return True\n+\n+\n+def run_loop(state: LoopState, args: argparse.Namespace | None, root: Path | None = None) -> LoopState:\n+    project_root = Path(root or state[\"config\"][\"project_dir\"]).resolve()\n+    plan_dir = _loop_dir(state[\"config\"][\"project_dir\"], state[\"name\"])\n+    save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+\n+    while should_continue(state):\n+        if state.get(\"phase\") != \"execute\":\n+            run_plan_phase(state, plan_dir, args, project_root)\n+            save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+            if not should_continue(state):\n+                break\n+        run_execute_phase(state, plan_dir, args, project_root)\n+        save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+\n+    if state.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and state.get(\"goal_met\"):\n+        state[\"status\"] = \"done\"\n+    elif state.get(\"status\") == \"running\" and int(state.get(\"iteration\", 0)) >= int(state.get(\"max_iterations\", 0) or 0):\n+        state[\"status\"] = \"completed\"\n+    save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+    return state\n*** End Patch\n"

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Build MegaLoop — a minimal iterative agent loop inside the megaplan codebase.

## What it is

MegaLoop automates the run → observe → diagnose → fix → run again cycle. It's NOT a literal while loop — it's an agentic process where the loop state machine drives the agent through phases, and sub-agents do the deep work. The main thread stays high-level: reading summaries, updating the plan, dispatching workers.

## Key design requirements

1. Two phases: plan and execute, cycling. The first plan phase captures the loop spec (goal, command, success criteria, allowed changes, optimization strategy, bug-finding approach, philosophy). Subsequent plan phases UPDATE the spec based on what the last execution found. Execute runs the command, observes results, spawns sub-agents to investigate/fix, and produces structured iteration results. Then back to plan.

2. State machine grounding. The loop spec is a living document — it captures the goal, how we measure success, what strategies to use, known issues, tried-and-failed approaches, current best results. This is continuously updated as we iterate. It's the compressed context that makes iteration 50 as informed as iteration 1.

3. Sub-agents for all deep work. The main thread orchestrates. Sub-agents investigate failures, fix code, compare results. Reuse megaplan's existing worker dispatch (run_step_with_worker with Claude/Codex/Hermes routing).

4. Strong bias to continue. The default is to keep going. Only pause for genuine decision points (risky changes, genuinely stuck, goal met).

5. Git integration. Commit before each fix. If results are worse, revert. Branch tip = best known state. Like autoresearch.

## What to learn from

- MEGALOOP.md (/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/MEGALOOP.md): The vision doc. Key patterns: setup conversation → structured spec, per-iteration structured results, conversation checkpoints, context management via the spec as compressed history, sub-agents for depth.

- autoresearch (https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch): Radical simplicity. One file to edit (train.py), one metric (val_bpb), git as experiment tracking, program.md as the agent's instructions. The loop: edit → run → measure → keep/revert → repeat. Key insight: the constraint IS the design. Fixed 5-minute time budget makes all experiments comparable.

- megaplan itself: Reuse the worker dispatch, structured artifacts, CLI patterns. But DON'T replicate the full state machine complexity — megaloop should be much simpler. Two phases cycling, not seven phases in a pipeline.

## Constraints

- Lives in the megaplan codebase (new megaloop/ package or megaplan/loop/ subpackage)
- Reuses megaplan's worker infrastructure (hermes_worker, codex, claude dispatch)
- Minimal new code — the intelligence comes from the LLM prompts, not complex Python
- The loop spec format should be simple (JSON)
- CLI: megaloop init, megaloop run, megaloop status, megaloop pause

## What I want from the plan

A concrete implementation plan that respects the actual complexity (state machine, sub-agent dispatch, structured artifacts, git integration) while keeping the code as small as possible. Two phases cycling. No over-engineering. Dig into both MEGALOOP.md and autoresearch to understand what patterns to steal and what to leave behind.

      "description": "Create megaplan/loop/engine.py \u2014 the core loop engine. Functions: (1) init_loop(name, goal, command, project_dir, args) -> LoopState \u2014 creates initial state, persists to .megaplan/loops/{name}/state.json. (2) run_loop_worker(step, state, plan_dir, args, root) -> WorkerResult \u2014 the adapter that builds prompt via build_loop_prompt, constructs shim dict with just config and sessions, calls run_step_with_worker with prompt_override. Updates session tracking back into LoopState after call. (3) run_plan_phase(state, ...) \u2014 calls run_loop_worker('loop_plan', ...), parses spec update, merges into state.spec. (4) run_execute_phase(state, ...) \u2014 runs user command via subprocess with time_budget_seconds timeout, captures stdout/stderr (truncated), calls run_loop_worker('loop_execute', ...) for diagnosis/fix, manages git commit/revert cycle per plan Step 3. (5) should_continue(state) -> bool \u2014 checks goal met, budget exhausted, agent requested pause, status == paused. (6) run_loop(state, ...) -> LoopState \u2014 outer driver alternating plan/execute. Persists state and per-iteration artifacts after each iteration.",

        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Build MegaLoop — a minimal iterative agent loop inside the megaplan codebase.

## What it is

MegaLoop automates the run → observe → diagnose → fix → run again cycle. It's NOT a literal while loop — it's an agentic process where the loop state machine drives the agent through phases, and sub-agents do the deep work. The main thread stays high-level: reading summaries, updating the plan, dispatching workers.

## Key design requirements

1. Two phases: plan and execute, cycling. The first plan phase captures the loop spec (goal, command, success criteria, allowed changes, optimization strategy, bug-finding approach, philosophy). Subsequent plan phases UPDATE the spec based on what the last execution found. Execute runs the command, observes results, spawns sub-agents to investigate/fix, and produces structured iteration results. Then back to plan.

2. State machine grounding. The loop spec is a living document — it captures the goal, how we measure success, what strategies to use, known issues, tried-and-failed approaches, current best results. This is continuously updated as we iterate. It's the compressed context that makes iteration 50 as informed as iteration 1.

3. Sub-agents for all deep work. The main thread orchestrates. Sub-agents investigate failures, fix code, compare results. Reuse megaplan's existing worker dispatch (run_step_with_worker with Claude/Codex/Hermes routing).

4. Strong bias to continue. The default is to keep going. Only pause for genuine decision points (risky changes, genuinely stuck, goal met).

5. Git integration. Commit before each fix. If results are worse, revert. Branch tip = best known state. Like autoresearch.

## What to learn from

- MEGALOOP.md (/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/MEGALOOP.md): The vision doc. Key patterns: setup conversation → structured spec, per-iteration structured results, conversation checkpoints, context management via the spec as compressed history, sub-agents for depth.

- autoresearch (https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch): Radical simplicity. One file to edit (train.py), one metric (val_bpb), git as experiment tracking, program.md as the agent's instructions. The loop: edit → run → measure → keep/revert → repeat. Key insight: the constraint IS the design. Fixed 5-minute time budget makes all experiments comparable.

- megaplan itself: Reuse the worker dispatch, structured artifacts, CLI patterns. But DON'T replicate the full state machine complexity — megaloop should be much simpler. Two phases cycling, not seven phases in a pipeline.

## Constraints

- Lives in the megaplan codebase (new megaloop/ package or megaplan/loop/ subpackage)
- Reuses megaplan's worker infrastructure (hermes_worker, codex, claude dispatch)
- Minimal new code — the intelligence comes from the LLM prompts, not complex Python
- The loop spec format should be simple (JSON)
- CLI: megaloop init, megaloop run, megaloop status, megaloop pause

## What I want from the plan

A concrete implementation plan that respects the actual complexity (state machine, sub-agent dispatch, structured artifacts, git integration) while keeping the code as small as possible. Two phases cycling. No over-engineering. Dig into both MEGALOOP.md and autoresearch to understand what patterns to steal and what to leave behind.

      "description": "Create megaplan/loop/engine.py \u2014 the core loop engine. Functions: (1) init_loop(name, goal, command, project_dir, args) -> LoopState \u2014 creates initial state, persists to .megaplan/loops/{name}/state.json. (2) run_loop_worker(step, state, plan_dir, args, root) -> WorkerResult \u2014 the adapter that builds prompt via build_loop_prompt, constructs shim dict with just config and sessions, calls run_step_with_worker with prompt_override. Updates session tracking back into LoopState after call. (3) run_plan_phase(state, ...) \u2014 calls run_loop_worker('loop_plan', ...), parses spec update, merges into state.spec. (4) run_execute_phase(state, ...) \u2014 runs user command via subprocess with time_budget_seconds timeout, captures stdout/stderr (truncated), calls run_loop_worker('loop_execute', ...) for diagnosis/fix, manages git commit/revert cycle per plan Step 3. (5) should_continue(state) -> bool \u2014 checks goal met, budget exhausted, agent requested pause, status == paused. (6) run_loop(state, ...) -> LoopState \u2014 outer driver alternating plan/execute. Persists state and per-iteration artifacts after each iteration.",

megaplan.loop.engine import init_loop, load_loop_state, run_loop, run_plan_phase, save_loop_state\n+from megaplan.types import CliError, StepResponse\n+\n+\n+def _project_dir(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> Path:\n+    return Path(getattr(args, \"project_dir\", None) or root).expanduser().resolve()\n+\n+\n+def _loop_dir(project_dir: Path, name: str) -> Path:\n+    return project_dir / \".megaplan\" / \"loops\" / name\n+\n+\n+def _load_loop(project_dir: Path, name: str) -> tuple[Path, dict[str, Any]]:\n+    plan_dir = _loop_dir(project_dir, name)\n+    if not (plan_dir / \"state.json\").exists():\n+        raise CliError(\"missing_loop\", f\"Loop '{name}' does not exist in {project_dir}\")\n+    return plan_dir, load_loop_state(project_dir, name)\n+\n+\n+def _status_details(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+    results = state.get(\"results\", [])\n+    last_result = results[-1] if results else {}\n+    best_result = state.get(\"current_best\", {})\n+    return {\n+        \"iteration\": state.get(\"iteration\", 0),\n+        \"phase\": state.get(\"phase\", \"plan\"),\n+        \"status\": state.get(\"status\", \"unknown\"),\n+        \"best_result\": {\n+            \"outcome\": best_result.get(\"outcome\"),\n+            \"returncode\": best_result.get(\"returncode\"),\n+            \"metric\": best_result.get(\"metric\"),\n+        },\n+        \"last_outcome\": last_result.get(\"outcome\"),\n+        \"pause_reason\": state.get(\"pause_reason\"),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def handle_loop_init(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> StepResponse:\n+    ensure_runtime_layout(root)\n+    project_dir = _project_dir(root, args)\n+    if not project_dir.exists() or not project_dir.is_dir():\n+        raise CliError(\"invalid_project_dir\", f\"Project directory does not exist: {project_dir}\")\n+\n+    goal = str(getattr(args, \"goal\", \"\")).strip()\n+    command = str(getattr(args, \"command\", \"\")).strip()\n+    if not goal:\n+        raise CliError(\"invalid_args\", \"Loop goal is required\")\n+    if not command:\n+        raise CliError(\"invalid_args\", \"Loop command is required\")\n+\n+    timestamp = datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y%m%d-%H%M\")\n+    name = getattr(args, \"name\", None) or f\"{slugify(goal)}-{timestamp}\"\n+    plan_dir = _loop_dir(project_dir, name)\n+    if plan_dir.exists():\n+        raise CliError(\"duplicate_loop\", f\"Loop directory already exists: {name}\")\n+\n+    state = init_loop(name, goal, command, str(project_dir), args)\n+    run_plan_phase(state, plan_dir, args, root)\n+    save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+    return {\n+        \"success\": True,\n+        \"step\": \"loop-init\",\n+        \"plan\": name,\n+        \"plan_dir\": str(plan_dir),\n+        \"state\": str(state.get(\"status\", \"running\")),\n+        \"iteration\": int(state.get(\"iteration\", 0)),\n+        \"summary\": f\"Initialized loop '{name}' and produced the first planning spec.\",\n+        \"artifacts\": [\"state.json\", \"loop_plan_v1.json\", \"loop_plan_v1_raw.txt\"],\n+        \"next_step\": \"loop-run\",\n+        \"details\": _status_details(state),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def handle_loop_run(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> StepResponse:\n+    project_dir = _project_dir(root, args)\n+    plan_dir, state = _load_loop(project_dir, args.name)\n+    final_state = run_loop(state, args, root)\n+    return {\n+        \"success\": True,\n+        \"step\": \"loop-run\",\n+        \"plan\": args.name,\n+        \"plan_dir\": str(plan_dir),\n+        \"state\": str(final_state.get(\"status\", \"unknown\")),\n+        \"iteration\": int(final_state.get(\"iteration\", 0)),\n+        \"summary\": f\"Loop '{args.name}' finished with status {final_state.get('status', 'unknown')}.\",\n+        \"artifacts\": [\"state.json\"],\n+        \"next_step\": None,\n+        \"details\": _status_details(final_state),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def handle_loop_status(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> StepResponse:\n+    project_dir = _project_dir(root, args)\n+    plan_dir, state = _load_loop(project_dir, args.name)\n+    return {\n+        \"success\": True,\n+        \"step\": \"loop-status\",\n+        \"plan\": args.name,\n+        \"plan_dir\": str(plan_dir),\n+        \"state\": str(state.get(\"status\", \"unknown\")),\n+        \"iteration\": int(state.get(\"iteration\", 0)),\n+        \"summary\": (\n+            f\"Loop '{args.name}' is {state.get('status', 'unknown')} \"\n+            f\"at iteration {state.get('iteration', 0)} in phase {state.get('phase', 'plan')}.\"\n+        ),\n+        \"artifacts\": [\"state.json\"],\n+        \"next_step\": None,\n+        \"details\": _status_details(state),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def handle_loop_pause(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> StepResponse:\n+    project_dir = _project_dir(root, args)\n+    plan_dir, state = _load_loop(project_dir, args.name)\n+    state[\"status\"] = \"paused\"\n+    state[\"pause_reason\"] = str(getattr(args, \"reason\", \"\") or \"Paused by user.\")\n+    save_loop_state(plan_dir, state)\n+    return {\n+        \"success\": True,\n+        \"step\": \"loop-pause\",\n+        \"plan\": args.name,\n+        \"plan_dir\": str(plan_dir),\n+        \"state\": \"paused\",\n+        \"iteration\": int(state.get(\"iteration\", 0)),\n+        \"summary\": f\"Loop '{args.name}' paused.\",\n+        \"artifacts\": [\"state.json\"],\n+        \"next_step\": None,\n+        \"details\": _status_details(state),\n+    }\n*** End Patch\n"

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Build MegaLoop — a minimal iterative agent loop inside the megaplan codebase.

## What it is

MegaLoop automates the run → observe → diagnose → fix → run again cycle. It's NOT a literal while loop — it's an agentic process where the loop state machine drives the agent through phases, and sub-agents do the deep work. The main thread stays high-level: reading summaries, updating the plan, dispatching workers.

## Key design requirements

1. Two phases: plan and execute, cycling. The first plan phase captures the loop spec (goal, command, success criteria, allowed changes, optimization strategy, bug-finding approach, philosophy). Subsequent plan phases UPDATE the spec based on what the last execution found. Execute runs the command, observes results, spawns sub-agents to investigate/fix, and produces structured iteration results. Then back to plan.

2. State machine grounding. The loop spec is a living document — it captures the goal, how we measure success, what strategies to use, known issues, tried-and-failed approaches, current best results. This is continuously updated as we iterate. It's the compressed context that makes iteration 50 as informed as iteration 1.

3. Sub-agents for all deep work. The main thread orchestrates. Sub-agents investigate failures, fix code, compare results. Reuse megaplan's existing worker dispatch (run_step_with_worker with Claude/Codex/Hermes routing).

4. Strong bias to continue. The default is to keep going. Only pause for genuine decision points (risky changes, genuinely stuck, goal met).

5. Git integration. Commit before each fix. If results are worse, revert. Branch tip = best known state. Like autoresearch.

## What to learn from

- MEGALOOP.md (/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/MEGALOOP.md): The vision doc. Key patterns: setup conversation → structured spec, per-iteration structured results, conversation checkpoints, context management via the spec as compressed history, sub-agents for depth.

- autoresearch (https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch): Radical simplicity. One file to edit (train.py), one metric (val_bpb), git as experiment tracking, program.md as the agent's instructions. The loop: edit → run → measure → keep/revert → repeat. Key insight: the constraint IS the design. Fixed 5-minute time budget makes all experiments comparable.

- megaplan itself: Reuse the worker dispatch, structured artifacts, CLI patterns. But DON'T replicate the full state machine complexity — megaloop should be much simpler. Two phases cycling, not seven phases in a pipeline.

## Constraints

- Lives in the megaplan codebase (new megaloop/ package or megaplan/loop/ subpackage)
- Reuses megaplan's worker infrastructure (hermes_worker, codex, claude dispatch)
- Minimal new code — the intelligence comes from the LLM prompts, not complex Python
- The loop spec format should be simple (JSON)
- CLI: megaloop init, megaloop run, megaloop status, megaloop pause

## What I want from the plan

A concrete implementation plan that respects the actual complexity (state machine, sub-agent dispatch, structured artifacts, git integration) while keeping the code as small as possible. Two phases cycling. No over-engineering. Dig into both MEGALOOP.md and autoresearch to understand what patterns to steal and what to leave behind.

    {
      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Create megaplan/loop/engine.py \u2014 the core loop engine. Functions: (1) init_loop(name, goal, command, project_dir, args) -> LoopState \u2014 creates initial state, persists to .megaplan/loops/{name}/state.json. (2) run_loop_worker(step, state, plan_dir, args, root) -> WorkerResult \u2014 the adapter that builds prompt via build_loop_prompt, constructs shim dict with just config and sessions, calls run_step_with_worker with prompt_override. Updates session tracking back into LoopState after call. (3) run_plan_phase(state, ...) \u2014 calls run_loop_worker('loop_plan', ...), parses spec update, merges into state.spec. (4) run_execute_phase(state, ...) \u2014 runs user command via subprocess with time_budget_seconds timeout, captures stdout/stderr (truncated), calls run_loop_worker('loop_execute', ...) for diagnosis/fix, manages git commit/revert cycle per plan Step 3. (5) should_continue(state) -> bool \u2014 checks goal met, budget exhausted, agent requested pause, status == paused. (6) run_loop(state, ...) -> LoopState \u2014 outer driver alternating plan/execute. Persists state and per-iteration artifacts after each iteration.",

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Build MegaLoop — a minimal iterative agent loop inside the megaplan codebase.

## What it is

MegaLoop automates the run → observe → diagnose → fix → run again cycle. It's NOT a literal while loop — it's an agentic process where the loop state machine drives the agent through phases, and sub-agents do the deep work. The main thread stays high-level: reading summaries, updating the plan, dispatching workers.

## Key design requirements

1. Two phases: plan and execute, cycling. The first plan phase captures the loop spec (goal, command, success criteria, allowed changes, optimization strategy, bug-finding approach, philosophy). Subsequent plan phases UPDATE the spec based on what the last execution found. Execute runs the command, observes results, spawns sub-agents to investigate/fix, and produces structured iteration results. Then back to plan.

2. State machine grounding. The loop spec is a living document — it captures the goal, how we measure success, what strategies to use, known issues, tried-and-failed approaches, current best results. This is continuously updated as we iterate. It's the compressed context that makes iteration 50 as informed as iteration 1.

3. Sub-agents for all deep work. The main thread orchestrates. Sub-agents investigate failures, fix code, compare results. Reuse megaplan's existing worker dispatch (run_step_with_worker with Claude/Codex/Hermes routing).

4. Strong bias to continue. The default is to keep going. Only pause for genuine decision points (risky changes, genuinely stuck, goal met).

5. Git integration. Commit before each fix. If results are worse, revert. Branch tip = best known state. Like autoresearch.

## What to learn from

- MEGALOOP.md (/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/MEGALOOP.md): The vision doc. Key patterns: setup conversation → structured spec, per-iteration structured results, conversation checkpoints, context management via the spec as compressed history, sub-agents for depth.

- autoresearch (https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch): Radical simplicity. One file to edit (train.py), one metric (val_bpb), git as experiment tracking, program.md as the agent's instructions. The loop: edit → run → measure → keep/revert → repeat. Key insight: the constraint IS the design. Fixed 5-minute time budget makes all experiments comparable.

- megaplan itself: Reuse the worker dispatch, structured artifacts, CLI patterns. But DON'T replicate the full state machine complexity — megaloop should be much simpler. Two phases cycling, not seven phases in a pipeline.

## Constraints

- Lives in the megaplan codebase (new megaloop/ package or megaplan/loop/ subpackage)
- Reuses megaplan's worker infrastructure (hermes_worker, codex, claude dispatch)
- Minimal new code — the intelligence comes from the LLM prompts, not complex Python
- The loop spec format should be simple (JSON)
- CLI: megaloop init, megaloop run, megaloop status, megaloop pause

## What I want from the plan

A concrete implementation plan that respects the actual complexity (state machine, sub-agent dispatch, structured artifacts, git integration) while keeping the code as small as possible. Two phases cycling. No over-engineering. Dig into both MEGALOOP.md and autoresearch to understand what patterns to steal and what to leave behind.

    "id": "T5",
    "description": "Create megaplan/loop/engine.py \u2014 the core loop engine. Functions: (1) init_loop(name, goal, command, project_dir, args) -> LoopState \u2014 creates initial state, persists to .megaplan/loops/{name}/state.json. (2) run_loop_worker(step, state, plan_dir, args, root) -> WorkerResult \u2014 the adapter that builds prompt via build_loop_prompt, constructs shim dict with just config and sessions, calls run_step_with_worker with prompt_override. Updates session tracking back into LoopState after call. (3) run_plan_phase(state, ...) \u2014 calls run_loop_worker('loop_plan', ...), parses spec update, merges into state.spec. (4) run_execute_phase(state, ...) \u2014 runs user command via subprocess with time_budget_seconds timeout, captures stdout/stderr (truncated), calls run_loop_worker('loop_execute', ...) for diagnosis/fix, manages git commit/revert cycle per plan Step 3. (5) should_continue(state) -> bool \u2014 checks goal met, budget exhausted, agent requested pause, status == paused. (6) run_loop(state, ...) -> LoopState \u2014 outer driver alternating plan/execute. Persists state and per-iteration artifacts after each iteration.",
